PRELIMINARY REMARKS 3. 


White working at this volume, в sunbeam glided into my study; on 
the 14% of February 1878 1 received аз a present, from Colonel Duncan, 
tbe British Resident at Mandalay, who had heard of my troubles 
from the Rev. C. H. Chard S. P. G. Missionary, а Burmese MS. of the 
whole Јаіака in 7 volumes, splendidly got up. This was indeed в 
liberal gift for which 1 cannot sufficiently thank the noble donor. 

Duncan's MS. agrees nearly throughout with B', so it seems 
evident that the Burmese MSS. in fact represent a redaction different 
from tbe Cingalese, and in some cases have most likely preserved 
the true readings, but notwithstanding this I shall, as [ have once 
resolved, still continue to give the Cingalese Redaction in the text. 
and put the Burmese readings in the footnotes. 

Being now furnished with two MSS. of the whole Jataka& and 
having had the promise of a continuation of a transcript from the 
Rev. W. Suhhüti, I hope to work with a little more ease, provided 
my good friends in Ceylon do not fail me. 

As it will appear from the Various Readings, [ have, besides 
the Kopenhagen MS. (C*), had a Transcript from Subhüti (C*) end 
the Burmese MS. of the India Office (B') for tbe Jatakas 151—160. 
For J. 161—220 1 had only C* and B', but as before M. L. Feer 
was good enougb to give me tbe readings of the Parisian MS. (ВР) in 
some of the difficult passages. For J. 221—300 1 have again had 
Subbüti's transcript besides Ck and В“, and up to J. 250 the con- 
tinued assistance of M. Feer. For J. 251-- 300 I have had the MS. 
presented to me by Colonel Duncan (B4) besides С“ C* and B', 

Kopeubagen, Febr. 20, 1679.
